## Recorded-Reward Interface Verified (Historical Stage, 2026-09-16)

The following pre-audit uncertainty and missing-value status describe the old
v1 sidecar. The new numerical audit above resolves the geometry question and
recovers original initial outcomes without rewriting that historical artifact.

The approved next step was supervision-interface preparation and label
availability, not training. Added `tools/pusht_recorded_reward_targets.py` and
`tools/prepare_pusht_recorded_reward_targets.py`; the 15,460 bytes of source
were SCP-transferred and remotely size-read back. The single CPU preparation
run completed in 0.277 s excluding startup/SSH. No video decode, model forward,
checkpoint load, optimizer/environment/hardware step or original label change.

The pinned `lerobot/pusht` snapshot has 25,650 records / 206 episodes with
`next.reward`, `next.done`, `next.success`; it does not retain exact coverage
or block pose. Reward is finite/nonconstant: 0..0.948879719, 10,396 unique
values; all success flags are false. The historical official raw converter
calculates clipped coverage/0.95, shifts outcome fields one frame forward and
repeats the final value. All 206 observed tails agree: reward/success repeat,
and exactly the last two rows have done=true (412 total). Done is not success
and its duplicate final row must not become another executed transition.
This historical source plus tail consistency supports the documented timing
convention, but does not identify/reproduce this snapshot's exact converter
runtime or establish equivalence to today's native coverage. See plan §15
for the official pinned source and this distinction. Do not inverse-rescale
the reward and call it verified current-environment geometric truth.

Prepared a separate supervision sidecar under
`simulation_output/pusht_recorded_reward_targets_v1/` on both machines:
`manifest.json`, `targets.npz`, `report.json`. It leaves the original cache,
split, goal, normalization, DS0/residual code/weights and ACT/real10 unchanged.
The proposed scalar is **terminal recorded reward**, higher is better:
`grid[t], agentXY[t], action[t:t+8] -> next.reward[t+7]`, corresponding to
frame t+8 under the documented conversion convention. Reward/done/success,
future grids and row identifiers are excluded from the scorer input tuple.

| Availability / mapping check | Train | Validation |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Original eligible windows / terminal targets | 21,958 | 2,002 |
| Windows with available current reward for a delta | 21,778 | 1,982 |
| Missing initial reward (never zero-filled) | 180 | 20 |
| Targets using the repeat-padded final source row | 0 | 0 |

Target mean/std are 0.298411148 / 0.274104406, computed on the original
training windows only. One 16-window real batch confirms byte-value-equal
current grids, observable XY and action chunks against the old loader;
shapes `[16,24,24]`, `[16,2]`, `[16,1,8,2]`, with separate `[16,1]` labels.
The reward row alignment is exact and standardized labels are finite.
Episode-start current reward remains NaN plus an explicit false validity mask;
it is not read as a model input. Existing all-nine-visible window selection
remains unchanged, including its 42 training exclusions.

**Decision: recorded-reward interface ready; a clean task-alignment training
comparison is not yet ready and has not started.** Two issues remain:

1. Verify recorded rewards against original numerical block poses / exact
   conversion semantics before claiming native coverage-aligned supervision.
   Only the small raw numeric state and episode boundaries are needed for that
   next audit, not another video download or environment rollout. They are not
   present in this snapshot; no acquisition/reconstruction was performed here.
2. DS0 learned visual **improvement**, while this sidecar preserves all windows
   by exposing a **terminal** outcome. Those are ranking-equivalent for exact
   within-context values but are different regression targets. A controlled
   future comparison should pair terminal visual quality with terminal task
   reward using identical architecture, original cohorts, budget and candidates;
   alternatively recover initial rewards before comparing deltas. Do not
   silently drop the 180/20 rows, impute zero, or call a terminal-vs-delta change
   a pure goal-target ablation.

No new training entrypoint, candidate evaluation or visual artifact is needed
for this schema-only step. No old inspected candidate outcomes were used.
Keep ACT/real10; defer DS1, any network change and policy-benefit claims. Only
algorithm handoff/commands/plan were updated; data/SOFA/shared docs and weekly
log remain untouched. Implementation and one real-batch verification are
complete; this is not model-quality or real-system evidence.

## Goal Alignment Audit Complete (Historical Stage, 2026-09-16)

The approved follow-up was a saved-evidence audit, not another experiment or
training run. Added `tools/audit_pusht_goal_alignment.py`, synchronized the
12,715-byte entrypoint after SSH passed, then executed it once in 0.209 s
(excluding startup/SSH). It reads the same 57 eligible contexts / 20 seeds,
285 saved future RGBs, the original training goal and frozen scorer decisions.
No checkpoint load, model forward, optimizer/environment/hardware step or
source-data/label/goal change. Original costs, gains and regrets reproduce
exactly; no new exclusion or example replacement.

Source inspection confirms different objectives: the scorer uses quadratic
soft-Dice to the visible gray object in training episode 1 / frame 117, whereas
native `_get_coverage` uses geometric object/goal intersection divided by goal
area. The former is a demonstration-image proxy, not exact task geometry.

Coverage regret decomposes exactly as `(Cmax - Cproxy) + (Cproxy - Cmodel)`.
The second term is signed: a model error against the visual proxy may improve
coverage. These are descriptive accounting terms, not causal error percentages.
Seed-macro values on the unchanged 20-seed group:

| Model | Total coverage regret | Proxy-goal term | Signed model-ranking term |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| DS0 | 0.001316460 | 0.000763234 | 0.000553226 |
| Residual | 0.001401937 | 0.000763234 | 0.000638703 |

Of DS0's 5 harmful choices, 4 are suboptimal for its own visual proxy where
the actual proxy oracle is non-harmful; 1 is proxy-optimal but harms coverage.
Residual has 3 and 2 respectively. DS0's worst harm, 300005/160, is a ranking
mistake: the proxy and coverage oracles both choose candidate 2, DS0 chooses 4.
DS0/residual are proxy-suboptimal in 11/9 contexts, and their signed ranking
terms are positive/zero/negative in 8/46/3 and 6/48/3 contexts. Improving proxy
prediction cannot be assumed to monotonically improve the benchmark metric.

The three actual-proxy harms remain even when all near-optimal proxy choices
within 1e-7 are considered; they are not an arbitrary tie-break artifact.
A fixed post-hoc resolution sensitivity check reuses exactly the same RGB
masks and goal at 96x96 instead of pooled 24x24. The same three harms remain,
four oracle choices change, and context-mean coverage regret is 0.000862554
vs 0.000803404. This does not support resolution alone as the fix; it does not
isolate goal-pose offset, occlusion, perception or objective form individually.
It is not a new trained model comparison or a new selected scoring function.

Evidence on both machines: `simulation_output/pusht_goal_alignment_audit_v1/`
has report/protocol, all 57 per-context rows and `goal_alignment_cases_zh.png`.
The figure includes the three previously reported proxy harms plus the previous
worst DS0 harm; it was inspected as `viewed_not_accepted`. Report keeps its
original `not_viewed`. No extra smoke, checksum sweep or numerical rerun.

Decision: both task-proxy mismatch and learned-ranking error exist. Retain
ACT/real10 and defer DS1; neither higher resolution nor more accurate old-proxy
prediction alone is an established remedy. Next proposed scope is to prepare
a task-aligned scoring target/data contract and verify training-label
availability, leaving the scorer structure unchanged. Then a separately scoped
same-budget comparison can test the target change. Exact coverage, if available,
is an offline simulation target only, never policy input or real-system truth.
Do not train on these inspected candidate outcomes and call them held-out;
action-distribution mismatch remains an untested hypothesis. No new training,
rollout, data/SOFA/shared-contract or weekly-log change was made here.

inspection, plus the plan and owning documentation. Zero model forwards,
optimizer/environment/hardware steps; no new scorer implementation or training.
The old27 eligible contexts have mean coverage-oracle headroom0.007416796852,
residual selected gain0.003460158696 and regret0.003956638156. The mean-gain /
mean-headroom ratio is46.65%, not success rate. Only7 contexts/5 seeds have
coverage differences above1e-6. Context200006/160 accounts for82.44% of regret;
200000/80 supplies89.40% of net gain. This is reused development evidence,
not a new test or proof that candidate coverage is sufficient everywhere.

Decision: hold the five native8-step candidates fixed and prepare a direct
short-horizon goal-effect scorer before adding more predictive machinery.
Supervise actual observed-action outcomes, not invented outcomes for perturbed
actions. Keep actual-future object-goal and coverage oracles distinct. VGAS
motivates generation/selection separation; its geometric prior is not observed
counterfactual truth. WCM's value-plus-prediction idea is a later auxiliary-loss
hypothesis, not an already implemented candidate-Q module.

proposal adapter would vary only single-step Elite translation while preserving
the original Piper head output and zero rotation; it must not be described as
an existing joint multi-step generator. No shared interface or controller change.

### ACT + Visual-Goal Adapter: Interface Only (2026-09-15)

Implemented `tools/pusht_world_model_adapter.py` and the single bounded check
`tools/check_pusht_world_model_adapter.py`. Existing BC/ACT training, inference,
protocol, checkpoints, real10 code and all data/SOFA-track files are unchanged
by this work. No new package or weight download. SSH to the same4090 succeeded.

Fixed interface for this preparatory version:

| Item | Shape / meaning |
|---|---|
| ACT observation | RGB `[B,3,96,96]` in0..1 and observable agent XY `[B,2]`; exact existing allowlist |
| Reference | First8 actions of frozen final-step100000 ACT chunk16, using the original postprocessor |
| Candidates | `[B,5,8,2]`: reference, +X, -X, +Y, -Y ramp offsets; raw absolute native targets0..512 |
| Offset fixture | At step h=0..7 add `(h+1)/8 * direction * 8`; declared coordinate units, not calibrated or tuned |
| Current / goal features | `[B,9,512]`: frozen ACT ResNet18 spatial3x3 map, row-major, ImageNet96 preprocessing; image-only |
| Future predictor input | Current visual features, raw observable agent XY, candidate action chunks; no goal-state fabrication or simulator truth |
| Required prediction | `[B,5,8,9,512]`, one future feature grid after each native control step; no temporal subsampling assumed |
| Score output | `[B,5]` costs, selected index, native `[B,8,2]` chunk and first action; no executor |

Candidate0 is retained bit-for-bit. A whole alternative chunk is masked if any
coordinate is nonfinite/outside0..512; no clipping. An invalid reference stops
the request. Ties choose candidate0/lowest index. The adapter reads a fresh
deterministic chunk without consuming or resetting ACT's original action queue;
a future combined rollout needs its own explicit execute8 loop. Do not mix
per-step baseline queue consumption with planning calls and call it unchanged ACT.

The caller must explicitly provide goal RGB, encoded by the same frozen encoder
as predictions. No validation/future-frame goal lookup is implemented. A future
task goal must be fixed from an external goal image or declared training-only
demonstration, before evaluation; this check uses only current RGB as an
**identity fixture**, not as a task-success goal. Feature-space IDs bind the
already-audited ACT model identity, preprocessing and row-major layout. DINO,
LIBERO pooled features and these ACT features cannot be silently mixed.

Score: terminal spatial feature MSE,
`J_k = sum_p(w_p * mean_d((z_hat[k,7,p,d] - z_goal[p,d])^2)) / sum_p(w_p)`.
Default weights are uniform. Optional nonnegative patch weights must come from
the goal image only, never environment pose/segmentation/contact truth. There is
no robot-state, coverage, success or branch-truth term. This is a simple goal
score interface, not evidence that3x3 ACT features adequately represent the task;
background/agent appearance and coarse spatial resolution remain limitations.
No trained predictor or synthetic-prediction fallback is provided.

Compatibility review: the author [DINO-WM Push-T dataset loader](https://github.com/gaoyuezhou/dino_wm/blob/main/datasets/pusht_dset.py)
defaults to relative actions with additional scale100 and normalization; its
[Push-T config](https://github.com/gaoyuezhou/dino_wm/blob/main/conf/env/pusht.yaml)
enables velocity inputs. Its [visual transform](https://github.com/gaoyuezhou/dino_wm/blob/main/datasets/img_transforms.py)
uses224px and0.5 mean/std, unlike this96px ACT interface. Thus its weights are
not directly interchangeable with our absolute2D prior. The terminal score is
inspired by the author's [goal objective](https://github.com/gaoyuezhou/dino_wm/blob/main/planning/objectives.py),
but this visual-only ACT-feature interface is **not a DINO-WM reproduction**.

### Native Spatial Visual Dynamics: Fixed First Training (2026-09-15)

The user approved the next visual-dynamics/goal step. Implemented independent
`tools/pusht_visual_dynamics.py` and `tools/run_pusht_visual_dynamics.py`; no
changes to the frozen ACT, BC, real10 pair, shared guidewire interface or any
data/SOFA-track file. Only the small algorithm sources are SCP-synchronized.
No package/weight downloads or new environment interactions are required.

The model has380288 trainable parameters: frozen ACT3x3x512 features ->128D
per-patch projection, spatial3x3 mixing, shared action-driven GRU, and residual
feature decoding. Initial observable agent XY conditions the hidden state;
each of eight recurrent steps consumes its corresponding native absolute XY
target. Output is raw frozen-ACT feature space `[B,K,8,9,512]`. There is no future
frame teacher forcing, goal conditioning in the dynamics, success/contact head,
RGB decoder or fine-tuning of ACT. `predict_candidates` predicts valid chunks
only and leaves invalid slots NaN, which the existing goal scorer masks.

Protocol fixed before training:

- Same pinned public source and complete186/20 train/validation episodes as
  BC/ACT. All25650 RGB frames are encoded once with the frozen ACT; no source
  labels/splits are changed. Full temporal windows only:
  `z[t], state[t], actions[t:t+8] -> z[t+1:t+9]`, at10 Hz, horizon0.8 s.
  There are22000 train and2002 validation windows; none crosses an episode.
  Overlapping windows are not independent trajectories.
- Visual normalization: per-channel mean/std over all23488 training frames and
  nine patches only; std floor0.001, zero channels hit it. State/action moments
  reuse the existing train-only statistics. Validation contributes no fitted
  moment, target choice or checkpoint selection.
- Fixed seed20260915,10000 updates, batch64, AdamW lr0.0003,
  weight_decay0.0001, gradient norm clip1; all-eight-step normalized visual MSE.
  One final checkpoint only for assessment; periodic last.pt is recovery only.
  Do not tune the budget/encoder/candidate offsets against this run's validation.
- Comparison: same-window image-feature persistence, and the trained predictor
  with observed versus train-mean actions at inference. The latter is an input
  ablation, **not** a separately trained action-free baseline or causal proof.
  Report all8-step normalized MSE, terminal raw-feature MSE, all8 raw-feature MAE,
  and terminal visual-goal-cost absolute error, as window means, per-episode
  values and episode macros. No classification imbalance/XAI contribution.
- Efficiency: report parameter count, fixed optimizer budget and measured run
  time. Generalization is limited to this one fixed20-episode split/one training
  seed; no robustness, cross-task, guidewire or real-system conclusion. Future
  ACT versus ACT+WM rollout must separately account for its five-candidate
  inference cost and extra dynamics-training budget; no such rollout is part of
  this run. Lower prediction/goal-cost error alone does not establish policy gain.

Decision: **visual prediction is partially improved, action selection is not
validated**. Preserve ACT22/100; no automatic deployment/reranking or more
training. Goal-cost calibration worsened and action ablation is small; lower
latent MSE does not resolve either issue. Each training observation supplies
only its recorded action sequence, not counterfactual labels for the four
offset candidates; demonstration prediction accuracy does not validate their
relative ranking. The useful next test is a small fixed
ACT versus ACT+WM closed-loop diagnostic on public Push-T, retaining the exact
prior/reference candidate and reporting the extra compute. That needs its own
executor and declared evaluation scope; none ran in this turn. It would test
whether action selection actually helps despite these mixed prediction metrics,
not justify more saturation/feature-statistics sweeps or real-system claims.

### ACT-Reference-Preserving Residual Gate: Training-Only Check (2026-09-15)

The user approved the bounded reference-preserving rule. Added independent
`tools/pusht_act_wm_abstention.py` and
`tools/calibrate_pusht_act_wm_abstention.py`. The completed greedy20-pair runner,
ACT/BC/dynamics architectures, losses, checkpoints, splits and real10 interface
remain unchanged. SSH succeeded; finalized small files were SCP-synchronized
and syntax-read back before execution. No package installation or large transfer.

Rule declared before reading the new residuals/candidate diagnostics:

```text
e(t) = abs(J(predicted z[t+8], fixed goal) - J(recorded z[t+8], fixed goal))
q = empirical95th percentile of e over all22000 training windows (method=higher)
threshold = 2 * q
keep ACT unless nonreference argmin improves predicted J by strictly > threshold
```

`J` stays terminal raw-feature MSE with uniform patches. The exact cached
training goal (episode1/frame117/index278), five candidates/8-unit ramp,
native absoluteXY bounds and chunk16/execute8 schedule are unchanged. The gate
returns the original ACT chunk or one existing candidate exactly: no blending,
clipping, new objective or silent fallback for invalid model predictions.
Ties, gains below the threshold and equality all preserve ACT. Calibration is
saved before inspecting candidate gains; no parameter sweep or later adjustment.

The scalar uses **in-sample observed-action residuals**, not independent
calibration. Overlapping windows receive equal weight, so long episodes count
more;22000 is not an independent sample count. The factor2 is an error-scale
heuristic, not a validated paired-action uncertainty bound or95% confidence
guarantee. Candidate errors may share/cancel bias, and their counterfactual
outcomes are not observed. Abstention cannot establish a safe-improvement theorem.
Existing loaders materialize shared train/val containers; only training indices
reach model inference/calibration here. No validation predictions, benchmark-log
reads, reward/success inputs, threshold fitting on the known20 outcomes, or
new environment interaction occurred.

### RGB Object/Goal Interface: Saved-Development Check (2026-09-15)

The next user approval implemented `tools/pusht_object_goal.py` and the bounded

Fixed feature space: `pusht_rgb_gray_T_occupancy24_v1`.

| Interface item | Frozen encoding |
|---|---|
| Observation | uint8 RGB96x96; image-only gray-object component |
| Pixel rule | R>=40; max(R,G,B)<=225; -10<=B-R<=70; abs(2G-R-B)<=40 |
| Component | Largest8-connected non-border component, at least16 pixels |
| Object/goal feature | float32 `[B,24,24]`,4x4 mean-pooling of binary mask |
| Geometry | Fixed image coordinates; X right/Y down; T silhouette retains orientation; no centering/rotation alignment |
| Relative metadata | Normalized centroid difference and visible-area ratio; not simulator pose |
| Future scoring interface | `[B,5,8,24,24]` grids plus five valid-candidate flags, including ACT candidate0 |
| Goal | Gray object in the unchanged training RGB: episode1/frame117/global278 |
| Terminal score | `sum((P-G)^2)/(sum(P^2)+sum(G^2))`; lower is better; exact ties choose lowest index |

This is a **handwritten task-specific RGB parser/scorer**, not a generic learned
representation or an algorithm-innovation claim. It does not read the green
target's geometry, simulator object pose, reward, coverage or segmentation.
The goal is the demonstrated object's visible pose, not the exact environment
target shape. No hidden object pixels are reconstructed. Detection validity is
not calibrated confidence. A missing component returns `valid=false`, a zero
grid and no centroid; it must not silently become a valid observed zero-object
state in a later predictor. No inference executor was added.

### Object-Grid Dynamics: Preparation Milestone (2026-09-15)

This section records the pretraining state; the subsequent authorized training
and final inspection are complete in the next section.

Fixed model/schema: `pusht_rgb_object_dynamics_v1`,248256 trainable parameters,
separate from ACT and from old WM checkpoints:

```text
current RGB -> frozen RGB rule -> [B,24,24] visible object grid
grid flatten -> Linear576->128 / LayerNorm / SiLU
observable native agentXY -> train-normalized Linear2->128
combined initial hidden + each train-normalized native action -> GRU128
Linear128->576 / sigmoid -> [B,K,8,24,24] predicted visible occupancy
```

Each future sees only the current grid/state and its action prefix. No future
teacher forcing, future agent state, goal, episode ID, coverage/contact/hidden
object geometry or diagnostic target enters the network. No ACT/old-WM weights
are loaded or modified. This is a small task-specific recurrent baseline, not
yet an innovation, learned dynamics quality or planner benefit claim.

Training protocol is declared in `PLAN` before any fitting:

- Same pinned `lerobot/pusht` revision7628202a and original186/20 complete-episode
  ownership. All25650 source RGBs are decoded with the existing pinned loader;
  no new dataset checks/hashes are added beyond reusing that loader. Only its
  image/state/action and episode/frame bookkeeping are read.
- Cache all frame positions as uint8 `[25650,24,24]` occupancy counts0..16,
  converted losslessly to float32/16. No learned visual normalization; retain
  the original full-training-only state/action mean/std without refitting on
  visibility-filtered rows. Goal RGB remains exactly train1/117/global278.
- Full8-step windows at10 Hz: `G[t], s[t], a[t:t+8] -> G[t+1:t+9]`. Exclude a
  window if any of its9 RGB-object observations is invalid; no padding, invented
  target, new labels, cross-episode stitching or resplit. Sampling is uniform
  with replacement over eligible training windows, not outcome-weighted.
- Loss is quadratic soft-Dice between each predicted and observed future grid,
  mean over all8 steps. Empty background does not dominate through a large
  count of correctly predicted zeros. The fixed goal is **not** a loss target.
  Seed20260915,10000 updates, batch64, AdamW lr3e-4, weight_decay1e-4,
  gradient clip1, float32/no TF32, fixed final10000 only; no intermediate
  validation, budget extension, checkpoint selection or threshold tuning.
- Final evaluation compares the predictor with current-grid persistence and
  the predictor fed unchanged train-mean actions (inference ablation, not a
  separately trained no-action baseline or causal proof), on the same eligible
  validation windows. Report all8 Dice/MSE, terminal Dice, fixed-goal-cost MAE,
  normalized centroid XY MAE and visible-area relative error; window means,
  per-episode values and episode macros. Prediction loss, goal-cost accuracy
  and future candidate ranking are different claims. No classification or XAI
  module, multi-training-seed stability, robustness or cross-task evaluation.

or contact physics and can saturate outside its interval. No performance or
ranking improvement is established by adding the skip path.

| Input / output | Fixed meaning |
|---|---|
| Current grid | RGB-derived occupancy `[B,24,24]` at t |
| `ObservableHistory.current_xy` | Native observed agent XY `[B,2]` at t |
| `previous_grid` / `previous_xy` | Same-episode, immediately preceding recorded t-1 observation |
| `valid` | Boolean `[B]`; previous frame exists, is consecutive and its object grid is valid |
| Missing history | Copy current grid/XY into previous slots and set valid=false; never fetch another episode or future |
| Actions | Unchanged native absolute XY `[B,K,8,2]` for t..t+7 |
| Output / targets | Unchanged `[B,K,8,24,24]` forecasts; actual t+1..t+8 grids are loss targets only |

Missing history produces an exact zero131-vector/zero added context. Available
but identical observations have valid=1, so they remain distinguishable from
missing history. These are one-recorded-frame differences, not measured physical
velocity. No new normalization fitting, previous-action input, goal input,
coverage/contact/hidden state, future teacher forcing or persistent state cache.

The ten sources contain 2,729 observations and 70 single feed requests. Retain
all 2,719 genuine next-observation transitions (70 feed, 2,649 hold). Each
episode's terminal observation/image is preserved, but its collector-generated
zero action has no next observation and is not used as an action target. No
`max_records`, quality pruning, temporal downsampling, or action relabeling.
All ten episodes are train-only for this first delivery; no internal validation
set, held-out metric, or best-checkpoint selection is implied.

Adapter version: `real10_pi05_observable_history_v1`. Zero-based, half-open
state layout:

| Dimensions | Input / encoding |
|---|---|
| 0:6 | Current measured Elite TCP pose, xyz in mm and rpy in rad |
| 6 | Previous record's `piper_step_after_command`; event count, NOT mm |
| 7:14 | Missing insertion length and unused tactile fields: zero |
| 14:16 | Left/right task one-hot, with the existing task instruction |
| 16 | Previous record's `piper_busy`, 0/1 |
| 17:27 | Unavailable full E2 event fields and validity bits: zero |
| 27 | Previous Piper history available: 1; episode reset/missing: 0 |
| 28:32 | Reserved padding: zero |

Only past controller snapshots enter the encoder. The current recorded
`piper_intent`, executed/requested command and user keyboard event are excluded;
they can encode the very target being predicted. Requested Elite pose/joints,
path index/status, timestamps, exact truth and estimator placeholders also do
not enter policy state. `async_started` is not relabeled as executed feed.
Missing history zeros dimensions6/16/27; no old simulation neutral-mean
imputation. Dimension27 is a real-adapter-specific validity bit in previously
reserved padding, not a claim that the complete E2 event schema is available.
Do not mix old E2 state encodings/checkpoint normalization into this prototype.

Output is unchanged: `elite_tcp_delta_6d + piper_intent_id`. Source Piper
`-1/0/1` maps explicitly to canonical `0/1/2` (retract/hold/feed); these ten
episodes contain only hold/feed. Compatibility `action_32[0:6]` holds the
unaltered stored delta and `[6:9]` the canonical one-hot. Rotation remains zero.
For the first real model use the existing Elite translation-only loss and
Piper state-only head, not a new architecture.

Images use the existing square224 OpenCV INTER_AREA preprocessing, stored as
lossless PNG with RGB conversion at loading. No crop, enhancement, noise,
depth input, or camera duplication. A live client must reuse the same resize
and one-step history reset/update rule. Source images/records stay read-only.

Actual record intervals are irregular (median0.225156 s, range0.132140–0.497936 s).
The pack retains image, pose, decision timestamps and actual measured-pose
action duration in its index; the LeRobot export preserves a copy as
`project2026_source_index.jsonl`. Export `--fps 5` supplies an indexing clock,
NOT a measured physical5 FPS rate. Do not treat the15 FPS camera setting as
the action rate or reinterpret deltas as velocities. The existing PI05
configuration already uses chunk_size=1 / n_action_steps=1. A future live
controller still needs an explicit execution cadence and bounds; this pack
does not authorize blindly replaying deltas at15 Hz.

### BC/ACT Trainer Preflight Completed (2026-09-14, Before User Run)

Implemented the public-data training adapter, trainer, checkpoint/recovery
helper and sequential pair launcher. The final zero-update preflight passed

Frozen protocol: `docs/pusht-bc-act-training-protocol-v1.json`. Both models use
the existing186/20 episode split, saved train-only normalization, batch64,
100,000 optimizer updates, initialization seed20260913 and the same stateless
per-update training-frame anchor sampler. No augmentation or pretrained weights.
AdamW: BC lr1e-3, ACT main/backbone lr1e-5; both weight decay1e-4, betas0.9/0.999,
epsilon1e-8 and gradient clipping10. No LR scheduler, AMP or TF32. ACT retains
the official chunk16/execute8, L1+10KL and original padding reduction; BC remains
single-action MSE. Equal update/frame-anchor budgets do not equalize losses,
future-target counts, parameter counts, FLOPs or wall time. Single-seed results
will be pilot evidence, not seed-robust architecture superiority.

All2,162 validation frames are monitored every5,000 updates, using the first
action from a fresh observation-only prediction(no target-conditioned VAE).
Metrics are native x/y MAE, frame-mean and episode-macro MAE, RMSE and unclipped
action bounds violations. Validation does not choose a checkpoint or trigger
early stopping: only step100,000 is selected. The existing DP67/100 stays an
external pretrained reference, not a same-data/budget comparison. Training does
not run any environment or change the frozen future100-seed/300-step schedule.

The adapter decodes the single AV1 video once into709,171,200 bytes of uint8 RAM
cache(about676MiB), no derived image cache on disk. All25,650 frames and exact

## Scope Boundary

This track may change:

- PI05/OpenPI adapters and model wrappers;
- pretrained checkpoint loading and provenance checks;
- Elite continuous and Piper discrete action heads;
- state/tactile context injection into the model;
- algorithm-side losses, ablations, and open-loop evaluation;
- algorithm-specific documentation and 4090 commands.

Without explicit cross-track scope, this track must not change the following;
no authorization substitutes for evidence supporting readiness claims:

- `simulation.collect_formal_tip_line_guidance` or simulator data generation;
- real data collection scripts or hardware procedures;
- the meaning of `elite_tcp_delta_6d`, `piper_intent_id`, or estimator fields;
- dataset labels, class definitions, or provenance without a cross-track
  interface decision;
- real-system readiness claims.

## Shared Contract

The policy-facing target remains:

```text
observation + task instruction -> Elite TCP delta + Piper discrete intent
```

Controller-owned execution remains outside the policy:

- Elite TCP delta -> IK -> joints;
- Piper intent -> bounded hold/feed/retract on a supporting backend; retract
  is approved under `docs/project-state.md` / Retract Capability;
- controller owns timing, cooldown, bounds, safety, and executed-command logs.

The LeRobot compatibility tensor is not the final action contract:

```text
action_32 dims 0:6  = Elite TCP delta
action_32 dims 6:9  = Piper one-hot compatibility slice
action_32 dims 9:32 = padding
```

Keep `elite_tcp_delta_6d` and `piper_intent_id` explicit in every algorithm
path. Do not interpret padded dimensions as controls.

Optional data-track observation:

```text
estimated_contact_risk_flag
```

This field means an image-derived early near-wall/contact warning, not a strict
contact label. It is provenance-aware and policy-disabled until the data track
reports a validated simulation/real calibration gate. Algorithm experiments must
not silently substitute it for `estimated_contact_flag`.

simulation_output/contact_risk_diagnostic_pack_v1

early-warning field with `policy_input_allowed=false`; exact MuJoCo targets are
stored separately and must never enter policy observations.

Required fields:

```text
observation.images.side
observation.images.top
observation.state
action
elite_tcp_delta_6d
piper_intent_id
task
episode_index
frame_index
```

The algorithm agent consumes this interface as provided. Data quality,
collection, class provenance, and simulator correspondence are owned by the
data track. Surface an interface problem with evidence instead of silently
rewriting labels or collectors.

## Current Algorithm Decisions

- Use an openpi/pi0.5-style reproducible baseline first.
- Prefer 3B or smaller when feasible; 7B is the early practical upper bound.
- Treat pi0.7-style ideas as context-conditioning inspiration, not as a claimed
  reproduction.
- The first extra context channel is image-derived tactile/contact information.
- Evaluate open-loop before any rollout-like test.
- Treat all current results as algorithm feasibility, not real-system
  validation.

## Verified Status

Dataset and framework path:

- Pi-style and OpenPI-compatible packs validate and train in smoke tests.
- The LeRobotDataset export and official loader path work on the 4090.
- The dataset preserves `action_32` plus explicit `elite_tcp_delta_6d` and
  `piper_intent_id` targets.

Official PI05 architecture path:

- Preprocess and forward/backward probes pass with LeRobot `0.4.4`.
- Verified configuration: `paligemma_variant=gemma_2b`,
  `action_expert_variant=gemma_300m`, `dtype=bfloat16`, frozen vision encoder,
  expert-only training, and gradient checkpointing.
- A 1000-step run reduced validation diffusion loss from `1.4696` to `0.3158`.
- Held-out sampling remained weak: Piper `0.5032` versus majority `0.5292`;
  Elite TCP-delta MAE mean `0.6283`, median `0.2451`.
- Increasing inference steps to 50 did not materially improve the result.

Important implementation findings:

- LeRobot `0.4.4` PI05 forward and sampling paths use images and language but
  do not consume `observation.state`.
- The current 1000-step checkpoint is not a pretrained PI05 fine-tune. The
  script constructs `PI05Policy(config)` directly and freezes the randomly
  initialized PaliGemma path with `train_expert_only=True`.
- The older 1000-step checkpoint remains random-base architecture/loss
  feasibility only; it must not be relabeled after the pretrained base became
  available.

Verified pretrained-loading implementation:

- `tools/pi05_pretrained_loader.py` now resolves a pinned Hugging Face snapshot,
  compares source/project architecture fields, remaps LeRobot PI05 keys,
  rejects shape mismatches or insufficient parameter coverage, and records
  loaded/missing/unexpected keys plus before/checkpoint/after fingerprints.
- Existing PI05 probe and training entrypoints now fail closed unless a
  pretrained source is provided or `--allow-random-init` is explicitly used
  for architecture-only diagnostics.
- The remote metadata probe verified public, non-gated repository
  `lerobot/pi05_base` at commit
  `7de663972b7817d2c4cf2d84c821153dfea772e9`.
- Its single `model.safetensors` is `14,467,165,872` bytes with blob id
  `822bdb619cecbe41045c54788df264e57ec03572`.
- A synthetic safetensors load smoke and the existing project preprocess-only
  probe both pass on the 4090.
- The pinned 14.5 GB checkpoint is downloaded in the 4090 Hugging Face cache.
- The checkpoint serializes PaliGemma `lm_head.weight` but omits the tied
  `embed_tokens.weight`. A dedicated probe verified identical shape, data
  pointer, and storage pointer in the instantiated model. The loader accepts
  only this exact verified alias and checks both tensors again after loading.
- Full offline load and one project-data forward/backward pass succeeded:
  `812/812` checkpoint keys matched directly, raw missing keys were `1`,
  effective missing/unexpected/shape-mismatch counts were `0/0/0`, raw
  duplicated-state coverage was `0.872895`, and unique-parameter coverage was
  `1.0`. Loss was `1.043866`; backward produced `208` gradient tensors with
  gradient L2 `39.2795`.
- Pretrained loading is now verified as an algorithm feasibility milestone. It
  is not evidence of policy quality or real-system validation.

Mixed-head v1:

- The first explicit Piper classifier uses PI05 image/language prefix
  embeddings plus normalized state while reusing the frozen Elite checkpoint.
- Interface smokes passed and produced a 2.1 MB head-only checkpoint.
- The full 1000-step run collapsed. Train/validation Piper supports were
  `[0, 1660, 1820]` and `[0, 60, 68]`; final accuracy `0.53125` equaled the
  majority baseline, balanced accuracy stayed `0.5`, and all final predictions
  were class 2.
- Do not run full open-loop evaluation on the collapsed v1 mixed-head
  checkpoint.

Controlled Piper-head ablation v2:

- `state_only` encodes the normalized 32D state into a 128D branch and provides
  the diagnostic lower-bound head.
- `prefix_state` separately projects the pooled PI05 image/language prefix and
  normalized state into 128D branches, then fuses the equal-width branches.
  It no longer concatenates raw 2048D prefix and 32D state features.
- Both training and open-loop evaluation entrypoints can now initialize from
  the fail-closed pinned pretrained loader. Legacy v1 mixed-head checkpoints
  remain readable but are not candidates for further tuning.
- Head-mode, pretrained-loading, checkpoint round-trip, and two-step
  project-data integration smokes passed on the 4090. Both integration smokes
  used revision `7de663972b7817d2c4cf2d84c821153dfea772e9` at unique-parameter
  coverage `1.0` with the same split and class counts.
- The 64-record smoke validation slice contained only one class, so its
  accuracy is not a performance result. Use the controlled full-run commands
  and comparison tool in `docs/algorithm-track-commands.md` before choosing a
  head.
- The full controlled comparison used identical episode-level splits,
  train/validation sizes `3480/616`, train Piper counts `[0,1660,1820]`, seeds,
  weighting, and pinned pretrained coverage `1.0`.
- `state_only` was better: its best step was 100 with accuracy `0.625`,
  balanced accuracy `0.6167`, and predicted support `[0,46,82]`, exceeding the
  `0.53125` majority baseline without single-class collapse.
- Dimension-balanced `prefix_state` did not help. Its best step was 800 with
  accuracy `0.5234`, balanced accuracy `0.5515`, and predicted support
  `[0,121,7]`; accuracy remained below the majority baseline. Best balanced
  accuracy was `0.0652` below `state_only`.
- Both heads degraded after their best step. The training script now preserves
  `best_mixed_head_policy.pt` using balanced accuracy, accuracy, CE, then
  earlier step as deterministic tie-breakers, while retaining the final
  checkpoint for audit.
- Current decision: reject this `prefix_state` fusion as the Piper candidate;
  carry forward the early-stopped `state_only` head. This is an algorithm/data
  feasibility result, not real-system validation.
- The selected `state_only` 100-step rerun exactly reproduced the original
  step-100 accuracy, balanced accuracy, CE, and confusion matrix. Its best and
  final head weights are identical, while checkpoint metadata correctly marks
  best-validation versus final-step provenance.
- The mixed-head open-loop evaluator now fails closed unless the evaluation
  base matches the head's training-time pretrained source, revision, status,

  step 100, pinned revision, and coverage `1.0`; its task metrics are not a

- Full 616-record open-loop evaluation confirmed the `state_only` Piper head is
  weak but non-degenerate: accuracy `0.5552`, balanced accuracy `0.5643`, and

- The unfine-tuned pretrained PI05 Elite output is not ready: raw 6D MAE mean

  the controller owns the fixed magnet orientation. Keep

## E2 Weighted-Overlay Diagnostic

The algorithm track now has a fail-closed adapter for
`simulation_output/e2_spatial_candidate_pack_v1`. This pack is accepted only
with explicit `--allow-diagnostic-overlay`; it remains
`policy_training_ready=false` and `formal_data_allowed=false`.

The adapter preserves the existing model and loss contracts:

- images, instruction, Elite TCP pose dims `0:6`, and task one-hot dims
  `14:16` are the only E2 values mapped into the existing policy input;
- unmapped state dimensions use the mean computed from the original 851-record
  training split, and all normalization statistics remain base-only;
- Elite action dims `0:6` and Piper compatibility dims `6:9` retain their
  existing meaning; hold/feed IDs `1/2` map to action dims `7/8`;
- offline `training_weight` controls sampling only; `sample_role` is available
  only after prediction for reporting;
- `diagnostic_targets.jsonl` is not opened by the adapter.

Verified remote smokes:

```text
policy / weight rows: 836 / 836, ordered one-to-one
checked images: 1672
episodes / source trajectories / families: 4 / 4 / 2
hold / feed action mapping: dim 7 / dim 8
base / overlay weight mass: 851 / 836
expected overlay draw fraction: 0.495554
Piper base-only class counts: [0, 400, 451]
Piper base-only balanced weights: [0.0, 1.06375, 0.943459]
diagnostic targets opened: false
```

Elite state-conditioned, Piper `state_only`, pretrained revision, loss,
validation split, seeds, and evaluation metrics are unchanged. The controlled
comparison uses fresh weighted-with-replacement branches for both baseline and
overlay so each Elite branch receives exactly 900 optimizer steps and each
Piper branch exactly 1000. Final-step checkpoints are compared to prevent
different early-stopping points from becoming a training-duration confound.
Overlay samples never enter validation or model selection.

The implementation and one-step smokes are interface evidence only. No
baseline-versus-overlay performance result exists yet, so there is no stable
cross-track conclusion and no weekly-meeting entry.

Decision: the current CABD/DABC E2 overlay has no stable overall benefit under
its declared approximately 50% draw mass. Stop expanding or tuning these
current families. Retain the fresh paired baseline and do not adopt the
overlay checkpoints. This does not reject future genuinely independent
geometry/recovery families, but they require a new data-side hypothesis rather
than more variants of CABD/DABC.

The optional role diagnostic was not run. It would evaluate the 836-record
training overlay in-sample, cannot select a model, and requires 1672 additional
PI05 record inferences across both checkpoint pairs, about 5.4 times the total
paired held-out evaluation workload. It cannot overturn the held-out no-go
decision.

## E2 Observable Event-State Adapter Mapping

The current-scene nominal bridge audit showed that the previous E2 run hid
observable controller event state behind base-mean imputation. This is a real
confounder for the earlier Piper transition result and part of the Elite
overlay conflict. It does not invalidate the controlled no-go result, but the
result must not be interpreted as a clean test of event-conditioned E2 data.

The algorithm-side E2 adapter now uses the previously reserved, all-zero base
tail without changing the 32-dimensional state or either action head:

```text
0:6    elite_tcp_pose_6d
6:14   existing base Piper/tactile slots; E2 uses base-mean imputation
14:16  task one-hot
16     piper_busy, boolean
17     piper_cooldown, boolean
18     piper_request_accepted, boolean
19     piper_executed_feed, finite scalar in [0, 1]
20     piper_event_age_steps / (1 + piper_event_age_steps)
21:25  event status one-hot: idle, delayed, executing, completed
25     controller event-state valid
26     event-age valid
27:32  reserved; base-mean imputation
```

Raw `piper_event_id` is ignored. Exact contact, wall distance, tip/route truth,
`diagnostic_targets.jsonl`, offline sample roles, and training weights do not
enter the state. A wholly absent controller-event object maps to zeros in
`16:27`, matching all 1005 current base records. A null event age maps to value
zero and age-valid zero. A partially present controller object, an unknown
status, a non-boolean flag, or an out-of-range executed-feed value fails
closed.

Local adapter/schema smoke passed on all 836 E2 records and 1672 images. Busy,
executed feed, event age, age validity, and all four status dimensions are
nonconstant. The current E2 pack has no positive cooldown or accepted-request
sample, so those two dimensions remain constant in real E2 data; synthetic
toggle checks prove the adapter wiring only and do not create data support.
The smoke also confirmed zero event-tail values for all 1005 base records and
that raw event id has no effect.

Do not resume E2 training yet. Data may use this fixed layout to build and
audit the E3a pack, including real variation in event-boundary controller
fields and family-level split integrity.

## Action-Effect World Model Pre-Training Stack

The current algorithm direction supersedes immediate E2 retraining. The data
track's next simulation pack is not ready, while the current ten real episodes
are reported by the user as action/Elite-tracking credible but visually
degraded by vessel oxidation. No new real or simulation record was opened for
this implementation milestone.

The historical senior real dataset is excluded from every current training,
validation, normalization, class-weight, and model-selection path. Its
single-view mismatch, inconsistent camera geometry, weak Elite-following
episodes, and branch-only success definition make it historical BC context
only.

The implemented algorithm is a short-horizon, action-conditioned latent world
model around PI0.5:

```text
PI0.5 visual latent history + observable state history + task
  + candidate elite_tcp_delta_6d/piper_intent_id chunks
    -> predicted future latent/state/action effect/branch trend/uncertainty
    -> candidate score and selection
```

It is not another Piper classification head. PI0.5 remains the action prior;
the new model predicts the consequences of each candidate action chunk and
participates in selection. The policy output contract remains
`elite_tcp_delta_6d + piper_intent_id`.

Implemented training-preparation components:

- frozen PI0.5 multi-view mean-patch latent extraction interface;
- PI0.5 `action_32` candidate conversion to the nine active compatibility
  dimensions without promoting padding to controls;
- four-observation/three-action episode-safe temporal windows;
- optional validity masks and fail-closed feature-pack schema;
- source-, episode-, and window-balanced mixed sampling with initial
  sim/current-real mass `75%/25%`;
- training-episode-only state/Elite normalization, neutral missing-state
  imputation with an explicit validity mask, and saved inference statistics;
- fixed observable event-state layout, streamed feature-array SHA256, and
  split-to-manifest fingerprint binding;
- five-fold current-real split for exactly five left and five right episodes;
- coverage-only oxidation visibility transform with no geometric warp, plus a
  paired clean/degraded frozen-latent interface and masked consistency loss;
- latent/state/guidance/branch/feed/uncertainty losses;
- candidate reranking with explicitly policy-ineligible no-guidance
  counterfactuals;
- trainer `--dry-run` that builds the complete loader/model/loss path but does
  not perform optimizer steps.

Exact tip/contact/wall/route truth, raw event id, diagnostic targets, offline
roles, and sample weights are rejected from policy input. A future accepted
simulation pack may expose privileged fields only through separate masked
offline supervision with declared provenance.

The local and Ubuntu 4090 synthetic contract smokes passed on 2026-09-02: 54
records, six episodes, 18 episode-safe temporal windows, finite
forward/loss/backward, correct candidate selection, exact `0.75/0.25` sampler
mass, five folds each holding one left and one right current-real episode,
source/family leakage rejection, training-only normalization, feature/split
fingerprint checks, privileged-field rejection, and trainer
dry-run with `training_started=false`. This is software interface evidence
only, not data or model-quality evidence.

A zero-optimization probe then passed through the installed LeRobot `0.4.4`
PI0.5 path using one record from the existing 1005-record stride-5 simulation
baseline. Pinned revision
`7de663972b7817d2c4cf2d84c821153dfea772e9` loaded with effective parameter
coverage `1.0`; frozen Side/Top image embeddings produced finite latent shape
`[1,2,2048]`; the compatibility action converted to explicit active shape
`[1,1,1,9]`; `optimizer_steps=0` and `training_started=false`. This validates
the implemented PI0.5 feature interface against the existing baseline only;
it does not satisfy Gate A2 for the future sim/current-real feature pack.

Evidence:

```text
simulation_output/pi05_action_effect_world_model_smoke_v1.json
simulation_output/pi05_action_effect_world_model_smoke_remote_v1.json
simulation_output/pi05_action_effect_feature_interface_probe_v1.json
```

Detailed contract, losses, experiment ladder, and training gates:

```text
docs/vla-action-effect-world-model-plan.md
```

Training remains closed until the data track supplies an accepted source pack,
the user visually confirms representative source images, and a frozen real
PI0.5 feature-pack smoke passes provenance, split, mask, and latent-shape
checks.

## Baseline Ladder And Public Benchmark Gate

The user approved a controlled ladder from a senior-style weak BC through ACT,
Diffusion Policy, SmolVLA, and PI0.5. OpenVLA-OFT is excluded. The historical
senior dataset remains historical context only; a fair weak-BC row must rerun
the architecture on the same accepted data and split as the other project
models.

The new benchmark contract separates project diagnostics from public native
tasks:

```text
project_d0 / project_r0 -> 9D compatibility action ->
  explicit elite_tcp_delta_6d + piper_intent_id
pusht                  -> native continuous 2D action
libero_spatial         -> native continuous 7D action
```

Public action spaces are never reinterpreted as guidewire controls. Push-T is
the cheap visual continuous-control smoke; LIBERO-Spatial is the later
dual-view, language-conditioned benchmark. ACT/Diffusion must remain
task-specific on LIBERO rather than receiving a new language encoder inside a
baseline comparison.

Windows and 4090 contract smokes passed on 2026-09-11. The remote LeRobot
`0.4.4` probe imported ACT, Diffusion, SmolVLA, and PI05, and every policy
configuration accepted all four declared feature/action profiles. Zero-step
synthetic forward/backward also passed for ACT on project/Push-T, Diffusion on
project/Push-T, and a reduced random SmolVLA on the project profile. The
existing pinned PI0.5 feature probe remains its stronger execution evidence.
The final probe reports five of five lightweight executions passing and
`optimizer_steps=0`.

The missing SmolVLA processor dependency `num2words==0.5.14` was added to the
4090 environment; the subsequent final probe passed and `pip check` reported
no broken requirements. No pretrained SmolVLA policy weight was downloaded.

The deterministic noise contract provides evaluation-only Gaussian sensor
noise, contrast loss, blur, and occlusion at three severities. It preserves
image shape and geometry, uses paired episode/frame/view seeds, and rejects
privileged policy fields. Noise is not training augmentation and may not be
used for checkpoint selection in the first comparison.

The pinned public-environment dependencies were installed on the 4090 on
2026-09-11. The verified compatibility set is LeRobot `0.4.4`,
`gym-pusht==0.1.6`, `hf-libero==0.1.4`, `pymunk==6.11.1`, and
`cmake==3.31.6`. CMake was held below 4 because the two EGL probe packages use
an older CMake policy declaration; both `egl-probe==1.0.2` and
`hf-egl-probe==1.0.2` were built with isolation disabled. The extras installer
also attempted unrelated upgrades, so the existing PI0.5-compatible
`transformers==4.53.2` / `tokenizers==0.21.4` pair and OpenCV `4.12.0.88` were
restored explicitly. The package versions alone are not sufficient for the
installed PI0.5 port: OpenPI's five official `transformers_replace` files are
also required for AdaRMS, precision handling, and read-only KV-cache behavior.
The public-environment install had overwritten those files. An intermediate
PI0.5 load therefore failed closed with 37 missing and 74 unexpected AdaRMS
keys and parameter coverage `0.999989`. The files were reapplied from the clean
`/home/zsw/openpi` commit
`15a9616a00943ada6c20a0f158e3adb39df2ccac`; all five installed hashes now
match that source, `GemmaRMSNorm.forward` again accepts `cond`, and the final
pinned load returns coverage `1.0` with zero effective missing, unexpected, or
shape-mismatched keys.

`pip check`, CUDA availability, Push-T/LIBERO/PI05 imports, and the repeated
zero-training architecture probe all passed. The final probe reports
`pusht_env=True`, `libero_env=True`, four of four policy rows passing, five of
five lightweight executions passing, and `optimizer_steps=0`.

The bounded public-environment smoke now passes Push-T end to end. It verifies
raw `pixels + agent_pos`, processed `observation.image + observation.state`,
native action shape `(2,)` with bounds `[0,512]`, RGB render, finite one-step
reward, and exact state/image replay after two independent environment builds
with seed `20260911`.

LIBERO-Spatial task 0 now passes the same bounded runtime gate. The accepted
run uses the explicit project asset directory
`/home/zsw/project_2026/simulation_output/libero_assets_v1`, two 256x256 camera
views, processed `observation.images.image + observation.images.image2 +
observation.state + task`, an eight-dimensional processed state, relative
seven-dimensional native actions in `[-1,1]`, finite RGB render and one-step
reward, and exact raw state/image replay after independent environment builds
with seed `20260911`. The task instruction is preserved by preprocessing.
Push-T and LIBERO therefore close Gate B2 for the selected runtime profiles.

The accepted tool uses an isolated non-interactive config under
`simulation_output/`, pins LIBERO's process-local asset lookup to the explicit
project directory, never downloads demonstrations, and fail-closes on an
incomplete asset tree or an effective-path mismatch. A rejected intermediate
LIBERO v2 run exposed that upstream `hf-libero` otherwise ignores the config
asset path and creates `/home/zsw/.cache/libero/assets`; that 407 MB duplicate
cache remains untouched pending explicit cleanup authorization. The accepted
v3 and combined reports did not trigger another download. No public
demonstration dataset, real record, policy/checkpoint, training loop, or
optimizer step was used. The pygame `pkg_resources` deprecation and missing
private robosuite macro warnings were non-blocking for these offscreen smokes.

The evaluation-only visual wrapper is now integrated at the intended boundary:
raw environment observation -> visual corruption -> LeRobot preprocessing.
It supports the contract's Gaussian sensor noise, low contrast, blur, and
occlusion at severities 1/2/3, accepts both nested raw keys and literal dotted
processed keys, derives deterministic draws from episode/frame/batch/view and
noise seed, and rewrites only declared image leaves. The dependency-light
local and remote tests pass all four corruptions with shape/dtype and
non-image-field preservation.

The final public-environment paired smoke used Gaussian sensor noise severity
2, seed `20260911`, and 20 fixed-action transitions per environment. Push-T and
LIBERO task 0 both pass all checks: independently reset clean/noisy source
observations remain exact before corruption; raw non-image leaves, processed
state/task, reward, termination flags, and native action contracts remain
exact; both LIBERO image views and the Push-T image change on every frame; and
duplicate corruption calls are byte-exact. Actions are fixed independently of
observation, so this is environment/pipeline evidence rather than policy or
benchmark performance. No dataset, policy/checkpoint, training loop, or
optimizer step was used.

The final Chinese clean/noisy reset comparison sheets were agent-viewed with
no missing glyphs, clipping, or overlap. Their visual status is
`viewed_not_accepted` pending explicit user acceptance. The fail-closed v1
report exposed unsupported literal dotted-key lookup, and the numerically
passing v2 report exposed clipped titles; both are superseded by v3.

Gate B3 is closed for ACT + Push-T. A reduced ACT with
11,286,402 randomly initialized parameters ran the official LeRobot chain
`preprocess_observation -> add_envs_task -> env preprocessor -> ACT
preprocessor -> select_action -> ACT postprocessor -> env postprocessor ->
step`. Its first real environment batch had image shape `[1,3,96,96]` and state
shape `[1,2]`; both normalized and native actions had shape `[1,2]`. The policy
completed all 20 requested transitions, every action was finite and within the
native `[0,512]^2` bounds, and the observed action range was
`165.3074..345.0386`.

Because no Push-T dataset or checkpoint was loaded, the processors use explicit
smoke-only statistics derived from the environment bounds: state mean/std
`[256,256]/[256,256]` and action mean/std
`[256,256]/[128,128]`. These are not dataset estimates and must not be reused as
training normalization. The run used no pretrained policy/backbone weights,
backward pass, optimizer, or training. It proves ACT/Push-T interface closure,
not policy quality, reward, success, robustness, or guidewire capability. The
Chinese four-frame rollout sheet was agent-viewed without missing glyphs,
clipping, or overlap and remains `viewed_not_accepted` pending user acceptance.

Gate B3 is also closed for Diffusion Policy + Push-T. A reduced random
Diffusion Policy with 12,357,890 parameters consumed the same real first-batch
shapes and completed 20 transitions through the official environment/policy
processor chain. After the first `select_action`, both state and image history
queues contained the required two observations and the four-action queue
retained three actions after its first pop. Every native action was finite and
inside `[0,512]^2`.

Diffusion's default state/action normalization is MIN_MAX, so this smoke uses
the explicit Push-T native min/max `[0,0]/[512,512]` for both features. These
are environment-bound smoke values, not dataset statistics. The random policy
hit both 0 and 512 during the episode because its normalized samples reached
the configured clipping range; this is valid interface behavior but strong
evidence not to interpret the rollout as policy quality. No dataset,
checkpoint, pretrained policy/backbone weights, backward pass, optimizer, or
training was used. The Chinese rollout sheet was agent-viewed without text or
layout defects and remains `viewed_not_accepted`; red target markers clipped at
the environment image boundary reflect saturated native actions rather than a
report-layout defect.

Gate B3 is now closed for reduced SmolVLA + Push-T as well. A randomly
initialized one-layer SmolVLA with 204,069,056 parameters completed 20
transitions through the same official environment/policy processor chain. The
Push-T profile permits a constant instruction, so the environment's native
empty task was explicitly replaced with `Push the T-shaped block to the
target.` before the SmolVLA newline and tokenizer steps. The first real batch
contained image `[1,3,96,96]`, state `[1,2]`, and 11 valid language tokens;
the two-action queue retained one action after its first pop. Every native
action was finite and inside `[0,512]^2`, with observed range
`98.5756..334.5440`.

The smoke ran with `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` and `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1`,
`load_vlm_weights=false`, two flow steps, one VLM layer, and one expert layer.
Only the pre-existing 4.7 MB SmolVLM config/tokenizer/processor cache was
required; the fail-closed cache audit found zero model-weight files before and
after the run. SmolVLA's default state/action normalization is MEAN_STD, so the
smoke-only Push-T values are state mean/std
`[256,256]/[256,256]` and action mean/std `[256,256]/[64,64]`; they are not
dataset statistics and must not be reused for training. No dataset, checkpoint,
pretrained weights, backward pass, optimizer, or training was used. The
upstream `preprocessor.json` deprecation warning remains non-blocking. The
Chinese four-frame sheet was agent-viewed without missing glyphs, clipping, or
overlap and remains `viewed_not_accepted` pending user acceptance.

Gate B3 is also closed for pinned PI0.5 + Push-T. The full
`lerobot/pi05_base` checkpoint at revision
`7de663972b7817d2c4cf2d84c821153dfea772e9` loaded from the existing local
cache with unique-parameter coverage `1.0`, one verified tied-weight alias,
and zero effective missing, unexpected, or shape-mismatched keys. The policy
has 3,616,757,520 parameters. Only chunk size, executed action count, and flow
inference steps were reduced to two to bound this interface smoke; model
structure and loaded weights were unchanged.

The official processor received the profile-allowed fixed instruction `Push
the T-shaped block to the target.` and encoded the two-dimensional native state
as a 32-bin padded state prompt. The first batch contained image
`[1,3,96,96]`, state `[1,2]`, fixed-length language tokens `[1,200]` with 147
valid tokens, and 32 state bins in the prompt. The two-action queue retained
one action after its first pop. All 20 native actions were finite and within
`[0,512]^2`; their range was `234.0602..270.4275`.

PI0.5 uses QUANTILES normalization. The state q01/q99 values are the native
Push-T bounds `[0,0]/[512,512]`; action q01/q99
`[192,192]/[320,320]` are conservative central bounds-derived smoke values,
not dataset quantiles. The run was fully offline and used no dataset, backward
pass, optimizer, or training. The two vision-embedding warnings are
non-blocking because strict post-remap checkpoint checks passed. This proves
checkpoint/processor/environment closure only, not reward, success,
robustness, guidewire capability, or a PI0.5 benchmark score. The Chinese
four-frame sheet was agent-viewed without missing glyphs, clipping, or overlap
and remains `viewed_not_accepted` pending user acceptance.

The language-conditioned LIBERO-Spatial task-0 Gate B3 slice is now closed for
both reduced SmolVLA and pinned PI0.5. Both policies consumed the native task
instruction `pick up the black bowl between the plate and the ramekin and place
it on the plate`, two camera views, the eight-dimensional LIBERO state, and
returned native seven-dimensional relative actions. The environment processor
emits `[1,3,256,256]` camera tensors; the benchmark feature declaration remains

(SmolVLA padded resize `512x512`, PI0.5 inference resolution `224x224`). The

The random reduced SmolVLA has 204,069,056 parameters, one VLM layer, one
expert layer, width multiplier `0.25`, and two flow steps. It preserved the
native task through tokenization as 20 valid tokens, retained one action in its
two-action queue after the first pop, and completed 20 transitions. All native
actions were finite and inside `[-1,1]^7`; the observed range was
`-0.5700..0.5663`. It ran fully offline with no cached model-weight file and no
optimizer. Its MEAN_STD values are explicit semantic smoke scales for the
eight state dimensions and action standard deviation `0.2`; they are not

### B4b User-Run Preparation And B4a Schedule Correction (2026-09-12)

The user assigned the approximately one-hour B4b run to themselves and asked
for the subsequent plan. No B4b policy evaluation has been started by the agent.
Use `tools/run_pi05_libero_b4b.py` instead of the previous direct CLI command.

Inspection of installed LeRobot `0.4.4` found `LiberoEnv.step()` calls
`self.reset()` at termination, while the next official rollout also calls
`env.reset(seed=...)`. Each reset advances `init_state_id`. Thus the old B4a
audit's generated `0..9` list and source substring checks did not prove which
states were evaluated. Keep the observed ten successes and accepted visual
artifact, but withdraw the claim that B4a verified the frozen `0..9` schedule.
There is no retrospective runtime trace of those indices. Its scope is now
task-0 success/integration evidence with schedule qualification.

The B4b entrypoint enforces the already frozen schedule before each official
rollout: `init_state_id = episode_seed - 1000`. It delegates policy loading,
processors, action inference, physics, and aggregation to the installed
LeRobot evaluator. The wrapper records hashes of actual payloads supplied to
`set_init_state`, seeded and automatic reset events, episode outcomes, effective
configs, source hashes, and checkpoint-file hashes. Policy RNG behavior stays
upstream (global seed 1000, no added per-episode torch reseeding).

The remote environment-only preflight passed. Actual `set_init_state` calls
were `0,1,1,2,1,2`: explicitly scheduled starts `0,1,1`, each followed by an
extra unseeded reset `1,2,2`. Out-of-range seeds were rejected. The repeated
seed correctly selected the same state; no policy construction, inference,
training, or B4b rollout occurred. Evidence:
`simulation_output/pi05_libero_spatial_b4b_preflight_v1.json`.

The long run writes live `run.log`, `run_manifest.json`, effective configs,
`episode_trace.jsonl`, videos, `eval_info.json`, and `status.json` under
`simulation_output/pi05_libero_spatial_b4b_v1/`. It runs in a user-started tmux
session so SSH disconnects do not stop it. An existing output directory is
never overwritten; a failed run can be retried in a separate attempt directory,
rerunning all tasks without pooling partial attempts or selecting the best score.
The final schedule/metric checks accept both successful and failed episodes.

### Plan After B4b

1. Audit the complete 10-task x 10-episode clean run against its runtime trace,
   report per-task success and the suite mean with counts, and inspect failure
   videos. Describe it as this project's frozen 100-episode LIBERO-Spatial
   protocol; equivalence to a published evaluation budget is not established.
2. Freeze that clean reference, then prepare paired `low_contrast` and `blur`
   evaluations at severity 2 first, keeping task/state schedules, checkpoint,
   action settings, and corruption seeds fixed. Report percentage-point and
   relative success drops. These are controlled visibility perturbations,
   not a calibrated measurement of vessel oxidation. Extend to all severities
   only after the initial comparison is verified.
3. Use accepted public demonstration splits to build the next world-model
   interface without waiting for new guidewire simulation: frozen PI0.5 visual
   features, observable robot state/history, and native LIBERO actions predict
   future visual features and robot-state changes. The current guidewire world
   model has project-specific 9D/Piper targets and ranking terms; it needs a
   public-task adapter before this experiment. Do not fabricate guidance or
   Piper labels for LIBERO. Verify windows, masks, task conditioning, and
   train-only normalization before a separately scheduled training run.
4. Test plain PI0.5, history/world-model prediction, added clean/degraded
   consistency, and candidate reranking as separate ablations. Check that
   shuffled/removed actions worsen prediction, then measure whether reranking
   improves held-out closed-loop success at a matched candidate/inference
   budget. Prediction accuracy alone does not establish policy improvement.
5. Return to fixed-split D0 and accepted current-real R0 once data-track source
   gates are met. Preserve the BC-to-VLA comparison ladder on matched data;
   current random model smokes cannot supply score-bearing baseline rows.
   Keep historical senior images excluded from current real-data mixtures.

## Native LIBERO World-Model Adapter Preparation (2026-09-12)

The user approved CPU-side adapter and temporal-window preparation while the
user-run B4b evaluation was in progress. This milestone changes no PI0.5
backbone, mixed head, guidewire model/loss, collector, simulator or data-track
document. It neither loads demonstrations/checkpoints nor starts training.

New implementation:

```text
tools/pi05_libero_world_model_adapter.py
tools/pi05_libero_feature_extraction.py
tools/prepare_pi05_libero_world_model.py
tools/test_pi05_libero_world_model_adapter.py
tools/test_pi05_libero_feature_extraction.py
```

The native contract is separate from `state_32` / Elite / Piper:

| Field | Fixed representation |
| --- | --- |
| State | 8D: EEF position XYZ, axis-angle XYZ, gripper qpos 0/1 |
| Action | Native relative 7D, finite `[-1,1]`, no clipping or Piper encoding |
| Cameras | `observation.images.image`, then `observation.images.image2` |
| Task | Original instruction; explicit source task index to Spatial task ID mapping |
| History / horizon | Default 4 observations / 3 actions and successor targets |
| Prediction targets | Two-view future latent and current-relative state coordinate residual |

The pinned `lerobot/libero` source revision
`a1aaacb7f6cd6ee5fb43120f673cebb0cfea7dd4` contains 40 tasks. Its source task
indices must not be assumed to equal Spatial IDs 0..9. The pack requires a
frozen exact instruction/source-index/Spatial-index registry; only the native
instruction enters model inputs. The synthetic test deliberately maps source
indices 20..29 to fixture suite IDs 0..9. Those invented fixture instructions
are not accepted native task texts or demonstration provenance.

At anchor t, observations t-3..t are inputs, actions t..t+2 are candidates, and
observations t+1..t+3 are targets. Full contiguous episode rows are required;
visible gaps, duplicate frames/resets, task switches and incomplete declared
episodes fail. No tail padding/repeated future frame is generated. A last-row
action without a recorded successor is excluded from windows and action stats.
State residuals subtract the current coordinate vector; axis-angle coordinate
subtraction is explicitly not an SO(3) relative-rotation target.

Missing state/view values require boolean masks. Invalid inputs become zero
after train-only normalization; target state validity intersects current,
future and training-supported coordinates. A coordinate absent throughout
training has mean 0 / std 1 and stays disabled even if observed in validation.
Observed constants use std 1. Actions/instructions are never imputed. World-model
statistics do not replace the frozen PI0.5 checkpoint processors' statistics.

Pack arrays are strict allowlisted `.npy` files, hash-bound and mmap-backed with
bounded finite-value scans. Splits must cover all declared episodes exactly,
be nonempty/disjoint, retain source/leakage groups in one partition, and bind to
the pack manifest. Both partitions cover the selected task registry. Validated
split mutation and foreign normalization are rejected; normalization is copied
at dataset construction. `inputs`, `targets` and provenance metadata remain
separate; reward/success/object truth/diagnostic/guidewire-label fields are not
model inputs. Source kind `evaluation_rollouts` is rejected: B4b is not a
world-model training source. Source identity, episode completeness and absence
of hidden resets are still manifest declarations until independent acquisition
audit, not facts established by a schema checker.

The extraction entrypoint accepts a caller-loaded eval-mode PI0.5 and two
floating BCHW images in [0,1]. It calls the policy's `_preprocess_images` exactly
once before frozen patch-mean embedding, preserving checkpoint resize/pad and
[-1,1] scaling. The pinned checkpoint's optional `empty_camera_0` is allowed
only with its declared empty-camera count, all-false mask and exact -1 pixels;
it is discarded before pooling the two real views. This batch API does not
load a policy, generate actions or perform gradients. CPU fake-policy tests
are not the still-pending real pinned-checkpoint feature probe.

Local and remote CPU smoke both passed 45/45 tests. The explicit synthetic
fixture has 180 rows, 20 episodes, 10 task mappings, and 30 training + 30
validation windows. It uses five-dimensional synthetic latents, not PI0.5
features. The remote run masked CUDA visibility and used one CPU thread.
Every uploaded small source file matched local SHA256 before execution.

optimizer_steps=0; training_started=false; training_ready=false

An initial local check found a report JSON serialization failure for NumPy
row IDs; IDs are now native integers and preparation closes mapped arrays
even on failure. The final full suite passed after the correction. Read-only
review also prompted copied normalization and chunked latent validation.

Implementation and synthetic/CPU verification are complete for this interface
scope. Public dataset acquisition/task-map verification, real frozen feature
generation, a native-action/task world-model implementation, training and
candidate ranking remain pending. The guidewire model still hard-requires 9D
actions; the new 7D batches must not be passed to its trainer. No world-model
quality, policy improvement or real-system claim follows. No visual behavior
was generated; unrelated rollout acceptance is not required for this schema
milestone. No group-meeting factual claim was appended for interface-only work.

Delegation: `/root/review_libero_adapter_contract` completed read-only contract
and code review; `/root/libero_feature_entrypoint` implemented only the new
image boundary and its fake-policy tests. Both used full-history forks with
inherited model/effort (exact settings unverified); neither ran SSH/GPU/training.

## B4b Independent Result Audit (2026-09-12)

The user authorized the result/trace/failure-video review. Observed success is
confirmed as **96/100 (96%)** for the pinned task-finetuned PI0.5 checkpoint:

| Spatial task | Successes / episodes | Failed seeds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0, 1, 2, 3 | 10/10 each | none |
| 4 (top drawer) | 9/10 | 1002 |
| 5 (on ramekin) | 8/10 | 1005, 1008 |
| 6, 7, 8 | 10/10 each | none |
| 9 (cabinet top) | 9/10 | 1001 |

Every failure reached the 280-step cap. The task macro mean equals the pooled
rate because all ten tasks have ten episodes. Upstream recorded `eval_s` is
477.087539 s (about 7.95 min), excluding earlier resource-check time; the
one-hour estimate was conservative. It is not a trained world-model result,
zero-shot claim or guidewire score.

The new offline audit independently passed 39 artifact/schedule/resource
checks locally and remotely, and 10 negative trace tests on each machine.
All 100 videos decode fully. The 492 trace rows comprise 196 actual applied
payloads, 196 resets and 100 completions. Each task executed seeds 1000..1009
with actual indices 0..9; 96 successful episodes each include one automatic
reset, while four horizon failures do not. The 110 unique task/index payloads
include automatic index 10. All six checkpoint/config/processor hashes,
versions, frozen protocol and runner match the preflight. The 107 original
run files remained unchanged through both audits.

**Do not label this a strict frozen-protocol pass.** All 100 episodes contain
at least one environment-input action outside the declared [-1,1] bounds;
global extrema are `-1.03310227394104` and `1.0188164710998535`. The trace stores
episode-wide extrema, not per-step/per-dimension arrays: neither the violating
dimensions nor saturation frequency is recoverable from it. Do not assert
that overshoot caused the four failures.

Read-only inspection of the installed evaluator confirms those recorded
actions are after policy/env postprocessing and are passed to `env.step`.
The native relative OSC controller calls `scale_action`, which clips to its
input bounds before scaling. Panda gripper formatting uses action sign and
clips its accumulated internal gripper state. These are upstream controller
semantics, not an added audit wrapper. The frozen contract's unqualified
`clipping_allowed=false` and raw bounded-action declaration must not be
retroactively declared satisfied. A subsequent explicit protocol decision
should distinguish raw policy actions, native controller saturation, and any
extra wrapper clipping.

The initial camera concern was corrected after direct video/source inspection.
Serialized `effective_env_config.json` says 360x360, but all 100 saved videos
are 256x256. `configs.LiberoEnv.gym_kwargs` omits those size fields; factory
forwards those kwargs, so runtime `LiberoEnv` uses default 256x256. Its render
path flips the raw main camera image without resizing. Thus 360 is unpropagated
configuration metadata, **not a proved 360x360 rendering error**. Policy input
feature declarations are 256x256, and internal image preprocessing is 224x224.
Dual-view policy boundary tensor shapes were not directly logged in B4b.
Audit local v1's `raw_env_resolution_hw` label conflated configuration and
runtime evidence; preserve it as superseded and use local/remote v2.

The saved video has S frames for S actions: upstream slices render history at
`done_index+1` despite including a reset frame. The post-final-action image is
not saved. The audit initially assumed S+1, rejected the first video, inspected
the pinned source and corrected its expectation before successful v2 audit.
No video or outcome was altered. Failure sheets show eight sampled frames per
episode, not continuous human/agent observation of every frame. Task 4 remains
around the drawer in the samples; task 5 includes transport and later
adjustment around the plate/stacked objects; task 9 carries the bowl toward
the plate but the late sampled placement remains in front of it. These are
descriptions, not definitive causal failure labels.

Local v2 failure sheets and the ten-success reference sheet were inspected;
